\forall y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\forall r\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\qquad\partial_{t}g_{t,y}^{Z}\left(P^{-1}r\right)=\partial_{t}g_{t,Py}^{X}(r),

and, for all
y,r\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
\partial_{y_{i}}g_{t,y}^{Z}(P^{-1}r)=\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{n}{P_{ki}\partial_{x_{k}}g_{t,Py}^{X}(r)}
. In particular,

\partial_{y_{1}}g_{t,y}^{Z}(P^{-1}r)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\nabla_{x}g_{t,Py}^{X}(r)\cdot\bm{1}\right).

In an analogous way, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}g_{t,y}^{Z}(P^{-1}r)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{k,\ell\,=\,1}^{n}{P_{ki}P_{\ell i}\partial_{x_{\ell},x_{k}}^{2}g_{t,Py}^{X}(r)}}=\Delta_{x}g_{t,Py}^{X}(r),

because
P
is an orthonormal matrix. From (
50
) and since
\left(P^{-1}x\right)_{1}=\frac{\left(x\cdot\bm{1}\right)}{\sqrt{n}}
, we deduce that the density
g_{t,x}^{X}
satisfies:

\forall t>0,\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\forall r\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\qquad\partial_{t}g_{t,x}^{X}(r)-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}g_{t,x}^{X}(r)+2\gamma(x\cdot\bm{1})\nabla_{x}g_{t,x}(r)=0.

Now, the fact that for all
f\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
,

T^{(n)}(t)f(x)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{f(r)g_{t,x}^{X}(r)\mathrm{d}r}

is
{\mathscr{C}}^{1,2}_{b}\left((0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and is a solution of
(
44
) on
(0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
follows from the theorem of differentiation under the integral sign. Note that, if
f
is continuous,

T^{(n)}(t)f(x)={\mathbb{E}}_{x}f\left(X_{t}\right)\xrightarrow[t\to 0]{}f(x)

by the dominated convergence theorem which leads to (
45
).

Step 3. Verification of (
44
) up to
t=0
.
Assume that
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
. This is equivalent to prove that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{f(u)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}-f(x)}{t}=\frac{1}{2}\Delta f(x)-2\gamma\left(\nabla f(x)\cdot\bm{1}\right)\left(x\cdot\bm{1}\right).

Let
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be fixed. Using
Taylor
’s formula we obtain that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{f(u)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}-f(x)={\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}_{t}+{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}_{t}+{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}_{t},

where

{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}_{t}:=\displaystyle{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left([u-x]\cdot\nabla f(x)\right)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u},}

{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}_{t}:=\displaystyle{\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{(u-x)^{t}{\rm{Hess}}(f)(x)(u-x)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u},}

{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}_{t}:=\displaystyle{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{R_{x}(u)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u},}

where
R_{x}(u):=o\left(\left\|x-u\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)
. As
g_{t,x}^{X}
is the density of
{\mathcal{N}}^{(n)}\left(m_{t,x},\Sigma_{t}\right)
, where

\displaystyle x-m_{t,x}=\left(1-\exp\left(-2\gamma nt\right)\right)\frac{(x\cdot\bm{1})}{\sqrt{n}}\times\frac{\bm{1}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\underset{t\to 0}{\sim}-2\gamma(x\cdot\bm{1})t,

(51)

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,n\},\quad\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)_{ii}

\displaystyle\underset{t\to 0}{\sim}t,

it follows that

\displaystyle{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla f(x)\cdot\left[m_{t,x}-x\right]\right)=-\frac{\left(1-\exp\left(-2\gamma nt\right)\right)}{n}\left(\nabla f(x)\cdot\bm{1}\right)\left(x\cdot\bm{1}\right).

\displaystyle{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left(u-m_{t,x}\right)^{t}{\rm{Hess}}(f)(x)\left(u-m_{t,x}\right)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{2}\left(x-m_{t,x}\right)^{t}{\rm{Hess}}(f)(x)\left(x-m_{t,x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{n}{\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{2}{f(x)}\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)_{ij}}+\left(1-\exp(-2\gamma nt)\right)^{2}\frac{\left(x\cdot\bm{1}\right)^{2}}{2n^{2}}\bm{1}^{t}{\rm{Hess}}(f)(x)\bm{1}.

Therefore,

\lim_{t\to 0}{\frac{{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}_{t}+{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}_{t}}{t}}=-2\gamma\left(\nabla f(x)\cdot\bm{1}\right)\left(x\cdot\bm{1}\right)+\frac{\Delta f(x)}{2}.

Now, it remains to manage the
{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}_{t}
error term. Note that,

\displaystyle\forall\varepsilon>0,\exists\alpha>0,\forall u\in B(x,\alpha),\qquad\left|R_{x}(u)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon\left\|u-x\right\|_{2}^{2}.

\displaystyle\forall u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\backslash\,B(x,\alpha),\qquad\left|R_{x}(u)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\left\|f\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\nabla f\right\|_{\infty}\left\|u-x\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\rm{Hess}}f(x)\right\|_{\infty}\left\|u-x\right\|_{2}.

Let
\varepsilon>0
,
\alpha>0
and
t_{0}\geqslant 0
such that for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,
\left\|x-m_{t,x}\right\|_{2}\leqslant\frac{\alpha}{2}
. Let
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. Separating the domain of integration of the integral of
{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}_{t}
into
B(x,\alpha)
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\backslash\,B(x,\alpha)
, it follows from the
Young
and previous inequalities that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}_{t}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left\|u-x\right\|_{2}^{2}g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}+C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\backslash\,B(x,\alpha)}{\left(1+\left\|u-x\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\varepsilon\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)_{ii}}+2(\varepsilon+C)\left\|x-m_{t,x}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\backslash\,B(x,\alpha)}{\left(1+\left\|u-m_{t,x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

Now, for the choice of
\alpha
and then the
Markov
inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\backslash\,B(x,\alpha)}{\left(1+\left\|u-m_{t,x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,\backslash\,B\left(m_{t,x},\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)}{\left\|u-m_{t,x}\right\|_{2}^{2}g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\frac{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left\|u-m_{t,x}\right\|_{2}^{4}g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}}{\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\right)^{4}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{16n}{\alpha^{4}}\left(1+\frac{4}{\alpha^{2}}\right)\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left(u_{i}-\left(m_{t,x}\right)_{i}\right)^{4}g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}}.

As for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, the fourth moment of a random variable of law
{\mathcal{N}}\left(0,\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)_{ii}\right)
is smaller than
3\left(\Sigma_{t}\right)_{ii}^{2}
, it follows from (
51
) that there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}>0
such that

\frac{{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}_{t}}{t}\leqslant 2\varepsilon n+\varepsilon\widetilde{C}

for
t
small enough and then the conclusion.

Step 4. Expression of the derivatives of
T^{(n)}(t)f
.
Noting that for all
u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
g_{t,x}^{X}(u)=g_{t,0}^{X}(u-m_{t,x})
and using the symmetry property of this density, we obtain that

T^{(n)}(t)f(x)=\left(f\ast g_{t,0}^{X}\right)(m_{t,x}).

By the chain rule formula, we deduce the properties
(2)
and for all
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{2}T^{(n)}(t)f(x)=\left(\partial_{x_{j}}m_{t,x}\right)^{t}\left[\left(f\ast{\rm{Hess}}\left(g_{t,0}^{X}\right)\right)(m_{t,x})\partial_{x_{i}}m_{t,x}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+\left(\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{2}m_{t,x}\cdot\left(f\ast\nabla g_{t,0}^{X}\right)(m_{t,x})\right).

The property
(3)
follows. ∎

The following corollary is useful for bounding the dual process in Section
6.2
.

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
. We assume that there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,

|f(x)|\leqslant C_{1}\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right).

Then, for all
t>0
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, there exists two constants
C_{2}(t,n)>0
locally bounded on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{N}}
such that
t\mapsto C_{2}(t,n)
is non-decreasing and
C_{3}(t,n)>0
locally bounded on
(0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{N}}
satisfying

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\textbf{\rm{{(1)}}}&\left|T^{(n)}(t)f(x)\right|&\leqslant&C_{2}(t,n)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right)\\
\textbf{\rm{{(2)}}}&\left\|\left({\rm{Hess}}\left(g_{t,0}^{X}\right)\ast f\right)\left(m_{t,x}\right)\right\|&\leqslant&C_{3}(t,n)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right)\end{array}

Proof.

Step 1. Proof of (1).
From (
48
) and (
49
), note that for all
x,r\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
t\geqslant 0
,
g_{t,x}^{X}(r)=\prod_{j\,=\,1}^{n}{g_{t,\left[P^{-1}x\right]_{j}}^{Z_{j}}\left(\left[P^{-1}r\right]_{j}\right)},

where

\displaystyle g_{t,y_{1}}^{Z^{(1)}}(z_{1})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi e_{4}(t)}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left[z_{1}-y_{1}\exp\left(-2\gamma nt\right)\right]^{2}}{2e_{4}(t)}\right),

(52)

\displaystyle g_{t,y_{j}}^{Z^{(j)}}(z_{j})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left[z_{j}-y_{j}\right]^{2}}{2t}\right),\qquad j\in\{2,\cdots,n\}.

Since
\|Pz\|_{2}=\|z\|_{2}
for all
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we also have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\|u\|_{2}^{2n}g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\|z\|_{2}^{2n}g_{t,P^{-1}x}^{Z}(z)\mathrm{d}z}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\|u\|_{2}^{2n}g_{t,x}^{X}(u)\mathrm{d}u}

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{n-1}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{z_{i}^{2n}\prod\limits_{j\,=\,1}^{n}{g_{t,\left[P^{-1}x\right]_{j}}^{Z^{(j)}}(z_{j})\mathrm{d}z}}}=n^{n-1}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left[Z^{(i)}\right]^{2n}\right)}.

Classical moment bounds for Gaussian random variables show that
{\mathbb{E}}\left(G^{2n}\right)\leqslant C(n)t^{2n}
for
G\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,t)
and
C(n)>0
. Since
e_{4}(t)\leqslant t
and using (
52
), we deduce that there exists two constants
\widetilde{C}_{1}(n)
and

\widetilde{C}_{2}(t,n)
such that for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left[Z^{(i)}\right]^{2n}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{1}(n)\left(\left(P^{-1}x\right)_{i}^{2n}+t^{2n}\right)\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{2}(t,n)\left(1+\left\|P^{-1}x\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right).

The result
(1)
follows.

Step 2. Proof of (2).
Now, we want to control, for all
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
,

\left|\left(\left({\rm{Hess}}\left(g_{t,0}^{X}\right)\ast f\right)\left(m_{t,x}\right)\right)_{ij}\right|\leqslant C_{1}(n)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left|\partial_{r_{i}r_{j}}^{2}g_{t,0}^{X}(r)\right|\left(1+\left\|m_{t,x}-r\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right)\mathrm{d}r}.

For all
k\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
, we consider:

V_{k}(t):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}e_{4}(t)&{\rm{if}}&k=1\\
t&{\rm{if}}&k\neq 1\end{array}\right..

From (
52
), we deduce that for all
i,j,k\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\partial_{r_{i}}g_{t,0}^{Z^{(k)}}\left(\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(P^{-1}\right)_{ki}\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}}{V_{k}(t)}g_{t,0}^{Z^{(k)}}\left(\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{r_{j}r_{i}}^{2}g_{t,0}^{Z^{(k)}}\left(\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(P^{-1}\right)_{kj}\left(P^{-1}\right)_{ki}}{V_{k}(t)}\left(\frac{\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}^{2}}{V_{k}(t)}-1\right)g_{t,0}^{Z^{(k)}}\left(\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}\right).

Hence, for all
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\partial_{r_{j}r_{i}}^{2}g_{t,0}^{X}\left(r\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{n}{\frac{\left(P^{-1}\right)_{kj}\left(P^{-1}\right)_{ki}}{V_{k}(t)}\left(\frac{\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}^{2}}{V_{k}(t)}-1\right)g_{t,0}^{X}\left(r\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt+\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\,=\,1\\
\ell\,\neq\,k\end{subarray}}^{n}{\frac{\left(P^{-1}\right)_{kj}\left(P^{-1}\right)_{\ell j}}{V_{k}(t)V_{\ell}(t)}\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{\ell}g_{t,0}^{X}\left(r\right)}}

Noting that for all
i,j,k\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
,
\left|\left(P^{-1}\right)_{ij}\right|\leqslant 1
and
\left|\left(P^{-1}r\right)_{k}\right|\leqslant n\left\|r\right\|_{2}
,

\left\|m_{t,x}-r\right\|_{2}^{2n}\leqslant 2^{2n-1}\left(\left\|r\right\|_{2}^{2n}+2^{2n-1}n^{n}\left(2-\exp\left(-2\gamma nt\right)\right)\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right),

we deduce that for all
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
, there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}_{3}(t,n)>0
locally bounded on
(0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left|\partial_{r_{i}r_{j}}^{2}g_{t,0}^{X}(r)\right|\left\|m_{t,x}-r\right\|_{2}^{2n}\mathrm{d}r}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}{\left[\frac{1}{e_{4}(t)}\left(\frac{n^{2}\left\|r\right\|_{2}^{2}}{e_{4}(t)}+1\right)+\frac{n-1}{t}\left(\frac{n^{2}\left\|r\right\|_{2}^{2}}{t}+1\right)+n^{3}\left(\frac{1}{e_{4}(t)}+\frac{n-1}{t}\right)\left\|r\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times g_{t,0}^{X}(r)\left\|m_{t,x}-r\right\|_{2}^{2n}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{3}(t,n)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2n}\right).

The announced result
(2)
follows.
∎

6.1.2
MILD formulation

In this section, we establish the MILD formulation associated with the martingale problem (
17
) with
d=1
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
be a stochastic process whose law
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}
is solution to the martingale problem
(
4
)
with initial value
\mu
. Then, for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
,

\displaystyle\left\langle T^{(n)}(t_{0}-t)f,X_{t}^{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\gamma\int_{0}^{t}{\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\,=\,1\\
j\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left[\left\langle\Phi_{i,j}T^{(n)}(t_{0}-s)f,X_{s}^{n-1}\right\rangle-\left\langle T^{(n)}(t_{0}-s)f,X_{s}^{n}\right\rangle\right]\mathrm{d}s}}}

\displaystyle-\gamma\int_{0}^{t}{\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\,=\,1\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left\langle K_{i,j}T^{(n)}(t_{0}-s)f,X_{s}^{n+1}\right\rangle}\mathrm{d}s}}

is a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}-
martingale for
0\leqslant t\leqslant t_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
t_{0}\geqslant 0
. Using (
16
), let
u,v,w:[0,t_{0}]\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})\times\widetilde{\Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
be
{\mathcal{B}}([0,t_{0}])\otimes{\mathcal{B}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})\right)\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}-
measurable defined by

\bullet\ u(r,\mu):=\left\langle T^{(n)}\left(t_{0}-r\right)f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle
,

\bullet\ v(r,\mu):=-\left\langle\partial_{t}T^{(n)}(t_{0}-r)f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle
,

\bullet\ w(r,\mu):={{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}P_{T^{(n)}\left(t_{0}-r\right)f,n}\left(\mu\right)}
.

The expected result is a direct consequence of a version of

[
26
,
Lemma 4.3.4]
where we replace the assumption of boundedness on
w
by an assumption of domination :
\exists C>0,\forall t\in\left[0,t_{0}\right],\forall\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}}),\ w(t,\mu)\leqslant C\left(1+M_{2}(\mu)\right)
. Note that we have control on these moments (see Proposition
2.7
). The verification of the assumptions of this lemma is left to the reader. ∎

6.2
Proof of Lemma
3.6

Recall that, our goal is to prove , in dimension
1
, that the stopping time
\theta_{k}
, defined by

\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}},\qquad\theta_{k}:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|M(t)\geqslant k\quad{\rm{or}}\quad\exists s\in[0,t],\ \left\langle\xi_{s},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle\geqslant k\right\}},

satisfies
\lim_{k\to+\infty}{\theta_{k}}=+\infty
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}-
a.s. with
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
.
Before to prove Lemma
3.6
, we introduce the following lemma, whose proof will be given at Section
6.2.2
. We denote by
S_{t}
the number of jumps of the process
M
on the time interval
[0,t]
.

Lemma 6.4
.

If
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
then there exists a function
C_{0}
on
\bigcup_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}{(0,+\infty)^{k}\times\{k\}}
to
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, locally bounded, such that for all
\left(t_{j}\right)_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\in(0,+\infty)^{{\mathbb{N}}}
,
k\mapsto C_{0}\left(\left(t_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k},k\right)
is non-decreasing and satisfying

\forall t\in[0,T],\ \forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(t)},\qquad\left|\xi_{t}(x)\right|\leqslant C_{0}\left(\tau_{1},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{S_{T}+1}-\tau_{S_{T}},S_{T}\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2S_{T}}\right).

The bound obtained above will only allow us to show that
\theta_{k}\to+\infty

{\mathbb{P}}_{(\mu,\xi_{0})}-
a.s. under the assumption that the initial condition
X_{0}
has all its finite moments. The following remark shows that we cannot expect that
\theta_{k}\to+\infty
under weaker assumptions on the initial condition.

Remark 6.5
.

Let
\xi_{0}:x\mapsto\sin(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
and
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
. Let us assume that
\left\langle\left|{\rm{id}}\right|^{4},\mu\right\rangle=+\infty
and
\xi_{t}
successively jumps at times
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
and
\tau_{3}
with respective jump operator
K_{11}
,
K_{11}
and
\Phi_{13}
. If we denote by
\tau_{1,2}:=\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}
, straightforward (but tedious) computations give

\xi_{\tau_{1}}(x,y)=K_{11}T^{(1)}(\tau_{1})\xi_{0}(x,y)=-\exp\left(-\frac{e_{4}(\tau_{1})}{2}-4\gamma\tau_{1}\right)\sin\left(x\exp\left(-2\gamma\tau_{1}\right)\right)y^{2}

and the leading order term in
\xi_{\tau_{2}}(x,y,z)
is of the form
\left(ax-by\right)^{2}z^{2}\sin\left(cx+dy\right)
.
Now,

\xi_{\tau_{3}}(x,y)=\Phi_{13}T^{(3)}\left(\tau_{3}-\tau_{2}\right)\xi_{\tau_{2}}(x,y).

If
\tau_{3}=\tau_{2}
, we obtain as leading order term in
\xi_{\tau_{3}}(x,y)
the term
\left(ax-by\right)^{2}x^{2}\sin\left(cx+dy\right)
, which is not integrable with respect to
\mu^{2}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}y)
. If
\tau_{3}>\tau_{2}
, one can check that the leading order term in
T^{(3)}(\tau_{3}-\tau_{2})\xi_{\tau_{2}}(x,y,z)
is of the form
P_{4}(x,y,z)\sin\left(\widetilde{c}x+\widetilde{d}y+\widetilde{e}z\right)
where
P_{4}(X,Y,Z)
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
4
such that
P_{4}(X,Y,Z)\to\left(aX-bY\right)^{2}Z^{2}
,
\widetilde{c}\to c
,
\widetilde{d}\to d
and
\widetilde{e}\to 0
when
\tau_{3}\to\tau_{2}
. Therefore, for
\tau_{3}
close enough to
\tau_{2}
,
\xi_{\tau_{3}}(x,y)
has a non-zero term proportional to
x^{4}\sin\left(\left[\widetilde{c}+\widetilde{e}\right]x+\widetilde{d}y\right)
which is not compensated by another term. Hence,
\left\langle\left|\xi_{\tau_{3}}\right|,\mu^{2}\right\rangle=+\infty
if
\tau_{3}-\tau_{2}
is small enough, for any values of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
. Given
T
large enough, we have proved that
\theta_{k}\leqslant\tau_{3}\leqslant T
with positive probability.

6.2.1
Proof that Lemma
6.4
implies Lemma
3.6

Note that
\theta_{k}=\widehat{\theta}_{k}\wedge\widetilde{\theta}_{k}
where

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|M(t)\geqslant k\right\}}\quad{\rm{and}}\quad\widetilde{\theta}_{k}:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|\exists s\in[0,t],\ \left\langle\xi_{s},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle\geqslant k\right\}}.

Thanks to (
28
) it follows that
\widehat{\theta}_{k}\rightarrow+\infty
when
k\rightarrow+\infty
. In order to prove that
\widetilde{\theta}_{k}\rightarrow+\infty
when
k\rightarrow+\infty
, we rely on the control of the dual process obtained in Lemma
6.4
. So we need to control
\left\langle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}^{2S_{T}},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle
. Let
T>0
and
\varepsilon>0
be arbitrary. From (
28
), we choose
A:=A(T,\varepsilon)>0
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{(\mu,\xi_{0})}\left(S_{T}\leqslant A\right)\geqslant 1-\varepsilon/3
.
Then, using Proposition
2.7
, we choose
B:=B(T,\varepsilon,A)>0
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{(\mu,\xi_{0})}\left(\forall k\leqslant 2A,\ \forall t\leqslant T,\ \left\langle\left|{\rm{id}}\right|^{k},X_{t}\right\rangle\leqslant B\right)\geqslant 1-\varepsilon/3
. Finally, from Lemma
6.4
we choose
\overline{C}_{0}:=\overline{C}_{0}\left(T,\varepsilon,A\right)>0
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{(\mu,\xi_{0})}\left(C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant A},A\right)\leqslant\overline{C}_{0}\right)\geqslant 1-\varepsilon/3
.
We recall that for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
,
\left(\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{m}{x_{i}}\right)^{n}\leqslant m^{n-1}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{m}{x_{i}^{n}}
.
Thus, the following inequality

\displaystyle\left\langle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}^{2S_{T}},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle\leqslant M(s)^{S_{T}-1}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{M(s)}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M(s)}}{x_{i}^{2S_{T}}X_{t-s}^{M(s)}(\mathrm{d}x)}}

\displaystyle=M(s)^{S_{T}}\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{2S_{T}},X_{t-s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(M(0)+A\right)^{A}B,

takes place with probability
1-2\varepsilon/3
. Therefore, we deduce from Lemma
6.4
that for all
s\leqslant t\leqslant T
,

\left\langle\xi_{s},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle\leqslant\overline{C}_{0}\left(M(0)+A\right)^{A}B

In particular, for
k\geqslant\overline{C}_{0}\left(1+\left(M(0)+A\right)^{A}B\right)
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\widetilde{\theta}_{k}\geqslant T\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\{S_{T}\leqslant A\right\}\cap\left\{\forall k\leqslant 2A,\ \forall t\leqslant T,\ \left\langle{\rm{id}}^{k},X_{t}\right\rangle\leqslant B\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 98.16191pt\left.\cap\left\{C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant A},A\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant 1-\varepsilon.

The conclusion follows.
\square

6.2.2
Proof of Lemma
6.4

By mathematical induction on
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we prove the property

\displaystyle({\mathcal{P}}_{k}):\quad

\displaystyle\forall t\in\left[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}\right[,\ \forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(t)},\quad\left|\xi_{t}(x)\right|\leqslant C_{0}(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k},k)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2k}\right),

where
C_{0}
is locally bounded on
\bigcup_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\left(0,+\infty\right)^{k}\times\{k\}}
.

Initial case.
For
k=0
,
S_{0}=0
and
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
. Hence, the property
({\mathcal{P}}_{0})
is satisfied.

Inductive step.
We assume that, for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, the property
({\mathcal{P}}_{k-1})
is satisfied and prove that
({\mathcal{P}}_{k})
is also. Let
t\in\left[\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}\right[
and note that
M(t)=M\left(\tau_{k}\right)
. We make a partition of cases according to whether the dual process loses or gains a variable. Let
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right\}
be fixed.

Step 1. Case
\Lambda_{k}=\Phi_{i,j}
at the
k^{\rm{th}}
jump.
In this case,
M(\tau_{k})=M(\tau_{k-1})-1
and we deduce from the explicit expression (
25
) of the dual process that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)-1}
,

\displaystyle\xi_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=T^{\left(M(\tau_{k-1})-1\right)}(t-\tau_{k})\Phi_{i,j}T^{\left(M(\tau_{k-1})\right)}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1})\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x).

By using expression (
20
) of
\Phi_{i,j}
and the property
\left({\mathcal{P}}_{k-1}\right)
, we deduce from Corollary
6.2

(1)
that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\Phi_{i,j}T^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right)}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant C_{2}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right){C}_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k-1\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2(k-1)}\right),

where
C_{2}{C}_{0}
is locally bounded.
Using again Corollary
6.2

(1)
and the fact that
t\mapsto C_{2}\left(t,M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right)
is non-decreasing, we deduce the property
\left({\mathcal{P}}_{k}\right)
.

Step 2. Case
\Lambda_{k}=K_{i,j}
at the
k^{\rm{th}}
jump.

In this case,
M(\tau_{k})=M(\tau_{k-1})+1
and the explicit expression (
25
) of dual process that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1}
,

\displaystyle\xi_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=T^{\left(M(\tau_{k-1})+1\right)}(t-\tau_{k})K_{i,j}T^{\left(M(\tau_{k-1})\right)}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1})\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x).

From the expression (
21
) of
K_{i,j}
and Theorem
6.1

(3)
, we have for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1}
,

\displaystyle\left|K_{i,j}T^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1\right)}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=\left|\left(\partial_{x_{j}}m_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},\widetilde{x}}\right)^{t}\left[\left(\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}\ast{\rm{Hess}}\left(g_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},0}^{X}\right)\right)(m_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},\widetilde{x}})\partial_{x_{i}}m_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},\widetilde{x}}\right]\right|x_{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1}^{2},

where
\widetilde{x}=\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)}\right)^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)}
. From the property
\left({\mathcal{P}}_{k-1}\right)
and Corollary
6.2

(2)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|K_{i,j}T^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1\right)}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant C_{3}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right)C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k-1\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{2}^{2k}\right),

where
C_{3}{C}_{0}
is locally bounded.
Using Corollary
6.2

(1)
, we deduce the property
\left({\mathcal{P}}_{k}\right)
. We conclude by the principle of induction.
\square

6.3
Proof of Theorem
30

Recall that
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is a stochastic process whose law
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}
is a solution of the martingale problem (
4
) with
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\left(\xi_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
a dual process independent of
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
built on the same probability space. To simplify, we will note
{\mathbb{P}}={\mathbb{P}}_{(\mu,\xi_{0})}
the distribution of
\left(\left(X_{t},\xi_{t}\right)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
. As
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}})
and for the choice of the stopping time
\theta_{k}
given by (
29
), the set of quantities, involved in the expectations of the weakened duality identity (
30
), are bounded.

Step 1. Approximation reasoning.
To establish the relation (
30
) we introduce a increasing sequence
0=t_{0}^{n}<t_{1}^{n}<\dots<t_{p_{n}}^{n}=t
of subdivisions of
[0,t]
such that
t_{i+1}^{n}=t_{i}^{n}+h
with
h
tending to
0
. Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{t\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{0}^{M(t\wedge\theta_{k})}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{t\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{0},X^{M(0)}_{t\wedge\theta_{k}}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt=\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left[{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{t\wedge\theta_{k}-t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}}^{M\left(t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)\right.}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt-\left.{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{t\wedge\theta_{k}-t_{i}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}}^{M\left(t_{i}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{t_{i}^{n}\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)\right].

We are therefore interested in terms of the form

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{(s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{t\wedge\theta_{k}-(s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}}^{M\left((s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{(s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

(53)

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{t\wedge\theta_{k}-s\wedge\theta_{k}}^{M\left(s\wedge\theta_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right),\quad{\rm{for}}\ s\in\left[0,t-h\right].

It is sufficient to prove that these quantities are
O\left(h^{2}\right)
. The procedure to be adopted is as follows. First of all, we consider separately the two terms which constitute (
53
) in Steps 2 and 3. Then, we prove that the sum of these terms is
O\left(h^{2}\right)
in Steps 4 and 5. Throughout this section, in order to simplify the writing, the following notations are introduced:

t_{k}:=t\wedge\theta_{k},\qquad s_{k}:=s\wedge\theta_{k},\qquad{\rm{and}}\qquad s^{h}_{k}:=(s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}.

We note respectively
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
the first and second jump times after
s_{k}
for the process
M
. We denote by
\tau_{1,k}:=\tau_{1}\wedge\theta_{k}
and
\tau_{2,k}:=\tau_{2}\wedge\theta_{k}
.

Step 2. First term of
(
53
)
. We exploit the explicit expression (
25
) of the dual process and make the following partition:

(a)

If there has been
no
jump of
M
on the interval
\left[s_{k},s^{h}_{k}\right]
.

(b)

If there was
only one
jump of
M
on the interval
\left[s_{k},s^{h}_{k}\right]
and distinguish according to the events
\{\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}\}
and
\{\Lambda=K_{i,j}\}
where
\Lambda
is the first
\Lambda_{k}
defined by (
26
) and (
27
) after
s_{k}
.

(c)

If there are
two or more than two
jumps of
M
on the interval
\left[s_{k},s^{h}_{k}\right]
.

Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s_{k}^{h}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}^{h}}^{M\left(s_{k}^{h}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}^{h}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt={\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s^{h}_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{1,k}>s^{h}_{k}\right\}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s^{h}_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{1,k}\leqslant s^{h}_{k},\tau_{2,k}>s_{k}^{h},\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}\right\}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s^{h}_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{1,k}\leqslant s^{h}_{k},\tau_{2,k}>s^{h}_{k},\Lambda=K_{i,j}\right\}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s^{h}_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{2,k}\leqslant s^{h}_{k}\right\}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right).

We only deal with the second term, where there is a single jump with
\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}
. The other terms are treated in a similar way.

If there is a jump on
[s_{k},s^{h}_{k}]
and for
i\neq j\in\left\{1,2,\cdots,M\left(s_{k}\right)\right\}
fixed,
\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}
, then
M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)=M(s_{k})-1
and
\xi_{s^{h}_{k}}=T^{\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-\tau_{1,k}\right)\Phi_{i,j}T^{\left(M(s_{k})\right)}\left(\tau_{1,k}-s_{k}\right)\xi_{s_{k}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s^{h}_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{1,k}\leqslant s^{h}_{k},\tau_{2,k}>s_{k}^{h},\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}\right\}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\left\langle T^{\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-\tau_{1,k}\right)\Phi_{i,j}T^{\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)\right)}\left(\tau_{1,k}-s_{k}\right)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}^{h}}^{M\left(s_{k}\right)-1}\right\rangle\phantom{\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}+\gamma\left[\tau_{1,k}-s_{k}\right]M^{2}(s_{k})+\gamma\left[s^{h}_{k}-\tau_{1,k}\right]\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times{\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{1,k}-s_{k}\leqslant s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right\}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left.{\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}\right\}}{\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{2,k}-\tau_{1,k}>s^{h}_{k}-\tau_{1,k}\right\}}\right|\sigma\left(\tau_{1,k}\right)\vee\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right].

Now, using that, given
\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}
and
\sigma\left(\tau_{1,k}\right)\vee\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}
,
\tau_{2,k}-\tau_{1,k}
follows an exponential law of parameter
\gamma\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)^{2}+\gamma\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-2\right)
,
we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}\right\}\cap\left\{\tau_{2,k}-\tau_{1,k}>s_{k}^{h}-\tau_{1,k}\right\}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.\sigma\left(\tau_{1,k}\right)\vee\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{\exp\left(-\gamma\left[\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)^{2}+\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-2\right)^{\phantom{2}}\hskip-4.26773pt\right]\left[s_{k}^{h}-\tau_{1,k}\right]\right)}{M^{2}\left(s_{k}\right)+M\left(s_{k}\right)\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)}.

Then using that, given
\sigma\left(\tau_{1,k}\right)\vee\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}
,
\tau_{1,k}-s_{k}
follows an exponential law of parameter
\gamma M^{2}\left(s_{k}\right)+\gamma M\left(s_{k}\right)\left(M\left(s_{k}\right)-1\right)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s^{h}_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M\left(s^{h}_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\{\tau_{1,k}\leqslant s^{h}_{k},\tau_{2,k}>s_{k}^{h},\Lambda=\Phi_{i,j}\right\}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\gamma{\int_{s_{k}}^{s^{h}_{k}}{\left\{\left\langle T^{\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-r\right)\Phi_{i,j}T^{\left(M(s_{k})\right)}\left(r-s_{k}\right)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}^{h}}^{M\left(s_{k}\right)-1}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\phantom{\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{}}\right.}}

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt\times\left.\exp\left(-2\gamma\left(r-s_{k}\right)\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)-\gamma\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right)\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)\left(M(s_{k})-2\right)\phantom{\int_{s_{k}}^{s^{h}_{k}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}r.

Step 3. Second term of (
53
).
It follows from the MILD formulation of Proposition
6.3
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 1.42271pt={\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k}))}(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k})\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt+\gamma\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\left(\left\langle\Phi_{i,j}T^{\left(M(s_{k})\right)}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{M(s_{k})-1}\right\rangle\right.}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt-\left.\left.\left\langle T^{\left(M(s_{k})\right)}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\right)\mathrm{d}r\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt+\gamma\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{M(s_{k})}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\left\langle K_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{M(s_{k})+1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}r}\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}\right.\hskip-28.45274pt\phantom{\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{M(s_{k})}}\right).

Step 4. Conclusion.
Putting together all the previous equations, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{(s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{t\wedge\theta_{k}-(s+h)\wedge\theta_{k}}^{M\left([s+h]\wedge\theta_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{[s+h]\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt-{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle\xi_{s\wedge\theta_{k}},X_{t\wedge\theta_{k}-s\wedge\theta_{k}}^{M\left(s\wedge\theta_{k}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s\wedge\theta_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt={\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}+{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}+{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}+{\textbf{\rm{{(D)}}}}+{\textbf{\rm{{(E)}}}}+O(h^{2}),

where

(A)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k}))}\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\left.\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}-\gamma\left[s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right]M\left(s_{k}\right)\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\gamma{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle}\mathrm{d}r}\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right),

(B)

\displaystyle=\gamma{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\left\{\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k})-1)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}-r\right)\Phi_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})-1}\right\rangle\right.}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-4.26773pt\left.\phantom{\int_{0}^{s_{k}}}-\left.\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k})-1)}\left(0\right)\Phi_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{M(s_{k})-1}\right\rangle\right\}\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\phantom{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}}\hskip-28.45274pt\right),

(C)

\displaystyle=\gamma{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{M(s_{k})}{\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\left\{\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k})+1)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}-r\right)K_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})+1}\right\rangle\right.}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-4.26773pt\left.\phantom{\int_{0}^{s_{k}}}-\left.\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k})+1)}\left(0\right)K_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{M(s_{k})+1}\right\rangle\right\}\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\phantom{\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{M(s_{k})}}\hskip-27.03003pt\right),

(D)

\displaystyle=\gamma{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\left\{\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k})-1)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}-r\right)\Phi_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})-1}\right\rangle\right.}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-4.26773pt\left.\phantom{\int_{0}^{s_{k}}}\times\left.\exp\left(-2\gamma r\left[M(s_{k})-1\right]-\gamma\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right)\left[M(s_{k})-1\right]\left[M(s_{k})-2\right]\right)\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}\hskip-22.76228pt\right\}\mathrm{d}r\right),

(E)

\displaystyle=\gamma{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{M(s_{k})}{\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{\left\{\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k})+1)}\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}-r\right)K_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})+1}\right\rangle\right.}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-4.26773pt\left.\phantom{\int_{0}^{s_{k}}}\times\left.\exp\left(-2\gamma rM(s_{k})-\gamma\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right)M(s_{k})\left[M(s_{k})-1\right]\right)\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-14.22636pt\right\}\mathrm{d}r\right).

All these terms are
O\left(h^{2}\right)
uniformly with respect to the other parameters which is sufficient to conclude the proof. This can be proved similarly for each term, so we only give the details for
(A)
. We first notice that when
h\to 0
,

(A)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left(M(s_{k})\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)\int_{0}^{s_{k}^{h}-s_{k}}{\left\{\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k}))}\left(s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}\right)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\right.}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\left.\phantom{\int_{0}^{s_{k}}}-\left.\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k}))}(r)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{t_{k}-s_{k}^{\phantom{?}}-r}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\right\}\mathrm{d}r\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)+O\left(h^{2}\right).

By conditioning with respect to
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{s_{k}}
in the previous expression and using the MILD formulation of the Proposition
6.3
again we obtain that

\displaystyle{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}=\gamma{\mathbb{E}}\left(\exp\left(\gamma\int_{0}^{s_{k}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)M(s_{k})\left(M(s_{k})-1\right)\int_{0}^{s^{h}_{k}-s_{k}}{}\left[\int_{t_{k}-s_{k}-r}^{t_{k}-s^{h}_{k}}{\left\{\phantom{\sum\limits_{11}{}}\right.}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{M(s_{k})}{\left[\left\langle\Phi_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}\left(t_{k}-s_{k}-v\right)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{v}^{M(s_{k})-1}\right\rangle-\left\langle T^{(M(s_{k}))}\left(t_{k}-s_{k}-v\right)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{v}^{M(s_{k})}\right\rangle\right]\hskip-21.33955pt\phantom{\int_{0}^{s^{1}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt+\left.\left.\left.\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{M(s_{k})}{\left\langle K_{i,j}T^{(M(s_{k}))}(t_{k}-s_{k}-v)\xi_{s_{k}},X_{v}^{M(s_{k})+1}\right\rangle}\right\}\mathrm{d}v\right]\mathrm{d}r\right)+O\left(h^{2}\right).

Both integrals are on intervals of length at most
h
. Since all the quantities in the integrals are bounded by definition of the stopping time
\theta_{k}
, we deduce that
{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}\underset{h\to 0}{=}O(h^{2})
.
\square

Appendix A
Technical lemmas involved in the existence proof

A.1
Approximation lemma

Lemma A.1
.

Let
p,d\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\mu,\nu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\right)
,
F\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{3}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{p},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and
g=(g_{1},\cdots,g_{p})\in{\mathscr{C}}^{3}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\right)
. Then,

\displaystyle F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle\right)-F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{p}{\partial_{i}F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right)}\left\{\left\langle g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\left.\left\langle\nabla g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{t}\left\langle{\rm{Hess}}(g_{i})\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j\,=\,1}^{p}{\partial_{ij}^{2}F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right)}\left\{\left\langle g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle g_{j}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\left\langle g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle\nabla g_{j}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\left\langle g_{j}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle\nabla g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\left.\left(\left\langle\nabla g_{i}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\nabla g_{j}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right)\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip-9.95863pt\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt+O\left(\left\|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right\|^{3}+\sum_{j\,=\,1}^{p}{\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{2}{\left\|D^{k}g_{j}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\|^{3}}}\right)

where
D^{k}g_{j}
denotes the differential of order
k
of
g_{j}
.

Proof.

The general case
p,d\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
can be proved by a straightforward extension of the proof of the case
p=d=1
which is the only case that we prove. Applying
Taylor
’s formula to
g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle}=g\left(\cdot-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle\right)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle}-g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\left[g^{\prime}\left(x-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle\right)\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu-\mu\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}\left(x-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle\right)\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu-\mu\right\rangle^{2}+O\left(\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu-\mu\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left[\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle+\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\left[\left\langle g^{\prime\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle+\left\langle g^{\prime\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right]+O\left(\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right).

Therefore, we deduce the following approximation:

\displaystyle\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt=\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left[\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle+\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right]

(A.1)

\displaystyle\hskip 4.26773pt+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g^{\prime\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle+O\left(\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}+\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\left|\left\langle g^{\prime\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|\right).

Applying
Taylor
’s formula to
F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle\right)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle\right)-F\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=F^{\prime}\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right)\times\left[\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{F^{\prime\prime}\left(\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right)}{2}\times\left[\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+O\left(\left|\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right).

Using (
A.1
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left[\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}+\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-2\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+O\left(\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}+\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\left[\left|\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 24.18501pt\left.\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}+\left|\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle g^{\prime\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 24.18501pt\left.\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}+\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|\hskip-8.5359pt\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

and

\displaystyle O\left(\left|\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=O\left(\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}+\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\left[\left|\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|+\left|\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip-9.95863pt\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt\left.+\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|\left[\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}+\left\langle g^{\prime}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle^{2}\right]+\left|\left\langle g\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu\right\rangle},\mu-\nu\right\rangle\right|^{3}\right).

The announced result follows from
Young
’s inequalities. ∎

A.2
Lemma of convergence

Lemma A.2
.

Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and assume that
Y_{0}\in{\cal M}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfies

\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{k},Y_{0}\right\rangle<\infty
. We consider for
t\in[0,T]
, an increasing sequence
0=t_{0}^{n}<t_{1}^{n}<\dots<t_{p_{n}}^{n}=T
of subdivisions of
[0,T]
whose mesh tends to
0
. Then, for all
h_{1}\in\{1,{\rm{id}}\}
and
h_{2}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
, we obtain that, in
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
probability,

(1)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\int_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}{\left|\left\langle h_{1}\times\left[h_{2}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}-h_{2}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{s}\right\rangle}\right],Y_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{k}}\mathrm{d}s}=0,

(2)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\int_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}{\left|\left\langle h_{1},Y_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{k}\left|\left\langle h_{2}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}-h_{2}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{s}\right\rangle},Y_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{k}}\mathrm{d}s}=0.

Proof.

The two properties can be proved similarly. We only prove the first one. Thanks to Lemma
5.1

(1)(a)
and using that
h_{2}
is
Lipschitz
, there exists a constant
C_{{\rm{Lip}}}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}{\left|\left\langle h_{1}\times\left[h_{2}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}-h_{2}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{s}\right\rangle}\right],Y_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{k}\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leqslant\int_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}{\left|\left\langle h_{1},Y_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{k}\left[C_{{\rm{Lip}}}^{k}\left|M_{s}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right|\wedge\left(2\left\|h_{2}\right\|_{\infty}\right)^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s}.

If
h_{1}=1
, the dominated convergence theorem allows us to conclude the proof. If
h_{1}={\rm{id}}
, using that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}{\left|\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t}\right\rangle\right|}<\infty

{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
a.s. by Lemma
5.1

(1)(b)
, we can also apply the dominated convergence. ∎

A.3
Control of error terms

Lemma A.3
.

Let
t>0
be fixed and assume that
Y_{0}\in{\cal M}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfies
\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{2},Y_{0}\right\rangle<\infty
. Let
j\in\{0,1,2\}
and
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{4}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
fixed. The sequences

(1)

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left|M_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}\left({\rm{id}}\right)-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}\left({\rm{id}}\right)\right|^{3}}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

(2)

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left|M_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}\left(g^{(j)}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}\right)-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}\left(g^{(j)}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}\right)\right|^{3}}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

converge to
0
in
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
probability.

Proof.

Step 1. Proof of (1).
Let
\varepsilon>0
and
t\geqslant 0
fixed. Let
A>0
to be determined later. We introduce the stopping time

\tau_{A}:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{2},Y_{t}\right\rangle-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t}\right\rangle^{2}\geqslant A\right\}}

which satisfies almost surely
\lim_{A\to+\infty}{\tau_{A}}=+\infty
by Lemma
5.1
. Then, using
Markov
’s inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left|M_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right|^{3}}>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\tau_{A}\leqslant t\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\left\{\tau_{A}>t\right\}\cap\left\{\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left|M_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right|^{3}}>\varepsilon\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\tau_{A}\leqslant t\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left|M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t\wedge\tau_{A}}({\rm{id}})-M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}({\rm{id}})\right|^{3}\right)}.

From the definition of
\tau_{A}
, we obtain for all
s\in\left[t_{i}^{n}\wedge t,t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t\right]
,
\left|M^{{\rm{id}}}_{s\wedge\tau_{A}}({\rm{id}})-M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}({\rm{id}})\right|^{3}
is bounded and
\left\langle M^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right\rangle_{t\wedge\tau_{A}}
is
2\gamma A^{2}
-
Lipschitz
. Thanks to the
Burkolder-Davis-Gundy
inequality and Lemma
5.1

(2)
, there exists a constant
C_{1}
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left|M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t\wedge\tau_{A}}({\rm{id}})-M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}({\rm{id}})\right|^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left[\left\langle M^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right\rangle_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t\wedge\tau_{A}}-\left\langle M^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right\rangle_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right]^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}\left(2\gamma A^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t-t_{i}^{n}\wedge t\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Therefore, if we choose
A
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\tau_{A}\leqslant t\right)\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, and
n_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
n\geqslant n_{0}
,

\sqrt{\sup_{i\in\llbracket 0,p_{n}-1\rrbracket}{\left|t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t-t_{i}^{n_{0}}\wedge t\right|}}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2C_{1}\left(2\gamma A^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}t},

we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left|M_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}({\rm{id}})\right|^{3}}>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\tau_{A}\leqslant t\right)+\frac{C_{1}\left(2\gamma A^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\varepsilon}t\sqrt{\sup_{i\in\llbracket 0,p_{n}-1\rrbracket}{\left|t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t-t_{i}^{n}\wedge t\right|}}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leqslant\varepsilon,

and the first announced result follows.

Step 2. Proof of (2).
In similar way as previously, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{\left|M_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}\left(g^{(j)}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}\right)-M_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}^{{\rm{id}}}\left(g^{(j)}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}\right)\right|^{3}}>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,0}^{p_{n}-1}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left|M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t}\left(g^{(j)}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}\right)-M^{{\rm{id}}}_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\left(g^{(j)}\circ\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},Y_{t_{i}^{n}\wedge t}\right\rangle}\right)\right|^{3}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leqslant\frac{C_{1}\left(2\gamma\left\|g^{(j)}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\varepsilon}t\sqrt{\sup_{i\in\llbracket 0,p_{n}-1\rrbracket}{\left|t_{i+1}^{n}\wedge t-t_{i}^{n}\wedge t\right|}},

which converges to 0 when
n\to+\infty
.
∎

Appendix B
Extensions to the centered
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem

The goal of this section is to give some extensions to the martingale problem (
4
) which are equivalent and it is usual to switch from one to the other.

B.1
Extension to multiple variables

We firstly introduce the version of the centered
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem with
p\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
variables. We will then provide the heuristic for the martingale problem for polynomials given in the next section.

Definition B.1
.

The probability measure
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left(\widetilde{\Omega}_{d}\right)
is said to solve the
centered
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem with
p
variables
, with resampling rate
\gamma
and with initial condition
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\right)
, if the canonical process
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
on
\widetilde{\Omega}_{d}
satisfies
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}(X_{0}=\mu)=1
and for each
F\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{p},{\mathbb{R}})
and
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{p})
,

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{t}^{F,d}(g)

\displaystyle:=F\left(\left\langle g,X_{t}\right\rangle\right)-F\left(\left\langle g,X_{0}\right\rangle\right)

(B.1)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}{\sum_{j\,=\,1}^{p}{\partial_{j}F\left(\left\langle g,X_{s}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle\frac{\Delta g_{j}}{2},X_{s}\right\rangle+\gamma\left[\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{t}\left\langle{\rm{Hess}}\left(g_{j}\right),X_{s}\right\rangle{\rm{id}},X_{s}\right\rangle-2\left\langle\nabla g_{j}\cdot{\rm{id}},X_{s}\right\rangle\right]\right)}\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle-\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i,j\,=\,1}^{p}{\partial_{ij}^{2}F\left(\left\langle g,X_{s}\right\rangle\right)\left(\left\langle g_{i}g_{j},X_{s}\right\rangle-\left\langle g_{i},X_{s}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{j},X_{s}\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla g_{i},X_{s}\right\rangle^{t}\left\langle{\rm{id}}\,{\rm{id}}^{t},X_{s}\right\rangle\left\langle\nabla g_{j},X_{s}\right\rangle\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right.}

\displaystyle-\left\langle\nabla g_{i},X_{s}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle g_{j}\times{\rm{id}},X_{s}\right\rangle-\left.\left\langle\nabla g_{j},X_{s}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle g_{i}\times{\rm{id}},X_{s}\right\rangle\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip-9.95863pt\right)\mathrm{d}s

is a continuous
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}-
martingale in
L^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Omega}_{d}\right)
.

Theorem B.2
.

For all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\right)
, the probability measure
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}
constructed in
Theorem
2.4
, satisfies the martingale problem of
Definition
B.1
.

Proof.

We can deduce the result from the original
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem with
p
variables

[
10
]
given by (
B.2
) below, following exactly the same method as for the proof of Theorem
2.4
. We recall that, the probability measure
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left(\Omega_{d}\right)
is said to solve the
original
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem
with
p
variables with resampling rate
\gamma
and with initial condition
\nu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\right)
, if the canonical process
\left(Y_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
on
\Omega_{d}
satisfies
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}(Y_{0}=\nu)=1
and for each
F\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{p},{\mathbb{R}})
and
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{p})
,

\displaystyle M_{t}^{F,d}(g)

\displaystyle:=F\left(\left\langle g,Y_{t}\right\rangle\right)-F\left(\left\langle g,Y_{0}\right\rangle\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{\sum_{k\,=\,1}^{p}{\partial_{k}F\left(\left\langle g,Y_{s}\right\rangle\right)\left\langle\frac{\Delta g_{k}}{2},Y_{s}\right\rangle}\mathrm{d}s}

(B.2)

\displaystyle-\gamma\int_{0}^{t}{\sum_{i,j\,=\,1}^{p}{\partial_{ij}^{2}F\left(\left\langle g,Y_{s}\right\rangle\right)\left[\left\langle g_{i}g_{j},Y_{s}\right\rangle-\left\langle g_{i},Y_{s}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{j},Y_{s}\right\rangle\right]}\mathrm{d}s}

is a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
martingale. ∎

B.2
Heuristics leading to the martingale problem for polynomials

Our goal here is to study the
Doob
semi-martingale decomposition of polynomial functions of the centered
Fleming-Viot
process. To obtain the expression of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{d}
for polynomial functions
P_{f,n}
with
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}(({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
(see (
16
)),
we first look for this expression when
f
has product form. The previous martingale problem (
B.1
) gives the following result: for the choice of
F(\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n}):=\prod\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\alpha_{i}}
where
\alpha_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, noting that
F\left(\left\langle g_{1},\mu\right\rangle,\cdots,\left\langle g_{n},\mu\right\rangle\right)=P_{f,n}(\mu)
with
f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right):=\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{g_{i}(x_{i})}
and
g_{i}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, we deduce that for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{d}P_{f,n}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\left(\left\langle\frac{\Delta g_{i}}{2},\mu\right\rangle-2\gamma\left\langle\nabla g_{i}\cdot{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle+\gamma\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{t}\left\langle{\rm{Hess}}(g_{i}),\mu\right\rangle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\,=\,1\\
k\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left\langle g_{k},\mu\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\gamma\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
j\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left(\left\langle g_{i}g_{j},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g_{i},\mu\right\rangle\left\langle g_{j},\mu\right\rangle+\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{t}\left\langle\nabla g_{i},\mu\right\rangle\left\langle\nabla g_{j},\mu\right\rangle^{t}{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right.}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\left.\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}-\left\langle\nabla g_{j},\mu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle g_{i}\times{\rm{id}}\right\rangle-\left\langle\nabla g_{i},\mu\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle g_{j}\times{\rm{id}}\right\rangle\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\,=\,1\\
k\,\neq\,i,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left\langle g_{k},\mu\right\rangle}

The previous relation leads us to introduce, for each
n,d\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}(({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{n},{\mathbb{R}})
, the operator
B^{(n),d}
defined by (
19
). Indeed, again for the choice
f(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}):=\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{g_{i}(x_{i})}
with
g_{i}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle B^{(n),d}f(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\left(\frac{\Delta g_{i}(x_{i)}}{2}-2\gamma\nabla g_{i}(x_{i})\cdot{\rm{id}}(x_{i})\right)\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\,=\,1\\
k\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{g_{k}(x_{k})}}

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
j\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left(\left(\nabla g_{j}(x_{j})\cdot{\rm{id}}\left(x_{i}\right)g_{i}(x_{i})\right)+\left(\nabla g_{i}(x_{i})\cdot{\rm{id}}\left(x_{j}\right)g_{j}(x_{j})\right)\right)\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\,=\,1\\
k\,\neq\,i,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{g_{k}(x_{k})}}.

Note that, for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{d}{P_{f,n}(\mu)}

\displaystyle=\left\langle B^{(n),d}f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle+\gamma\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{t}\left\langle{\rm{Hess}}(g_{i}),\mu\right\rangle{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\,=\,1\\
k\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left\langle g_{k},\mu\right\rangle}}

\displaystyle+\gamma\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\,=\,1\\
j\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left(\left\langle g_{i}g_{j},\mu\right\rangle-\left\langle g_{i},\mu\right\rangle\left\langle g_{j},\mu\right\rangle+\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{t}\left\langle\nabla g_{i},\mu\right\rangle\left\langle\nabla g_{j},\mu\right\rangle^{t}{\rm{id}},\mu\right\rangle\right)}}\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\,=\,1\\
k\,\neq\,i,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left\langle g_{k},\mu\right\rangle}.

This leads us to introduce Definition (
3.2
) of the centered
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem for polynomials.

Appendix C
Regular conditional probabilities and Lemma
5.2

C.1
Technical result for Lemma
5.2

As the filtered probability space
\left(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}},\left({\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}\right)
is Polish (see Section
2.1
), we deduce from

[
30
,
Theorem 3.18 of Section 5.3 (p 307)]
there exists, for all
\nu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
, a unique family
\left({\mathbb{Q}}_{\omega}\right)_{\omega\in\Omega}
of regular conditional probability of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}
given
{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
and a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
such that for all
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N
,

{\mathbb{Q}}_{\omega}\left(\left\{\widetilde{\omega}\in\Omega\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right.\widetilde{\omega}_{t^{\star}}=\omega_{t^{\star}}\right\}\right)=1.

(C.1)

The following Theorem
C.1
ensures that time shifts of regular conditional probabilities of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}
remain solutions to the
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem (
8
). The proof of this result is given hereafter and is based on the proof of

[
30
,
Lemma 4.19 of Section 5.4 (p 321)]
. We introduce, for
\omega\in\Omega
, the time-shift operator
\theta
defined by

\left[\theta_{s}\omega\right]_{t}:=\omega_{s+t},\qquad 0\leqslant t<+\infty,\quad s\geqslant 0.

Theorem C.1
.

Let
t^{\star}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be a deterministic time. Then there exists a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
such that, for every
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N
, the probability measure

{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right):=\theta_{t^{\star}}\sharp\,{\mathbb{Q}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)

(C.2)

solves the martingale problem
(
8
)
with
\nu:=\omega_{t^{\star}}
.

Proof
.
Step 0. Preliminary results.
We denote by
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
the space of real functions of class
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
with compact support. It is well-known that the formulation of the martingale problem (
8
) for
F,g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
is equivalent to the one for
F,g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})

[
10
]
. The space
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
equipped with the norm
\|f\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}:=\left\|f\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|f^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|f^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty}
is separable. So, we can choose a dense countable family
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
, for the topology associated to the norm, that is to say

\displaystyle\forall F,g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}),\quad\exists\left(F_{n}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}},\left(g_{n}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{{\mathbb{N}}},\qquad F_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{{\|\cdot\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}}}F,\quad g_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{{\|\cdot\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}}}g.

Hence, if we denote
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}:={\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}^{{1}}
we deduce that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}{\left(F_{n}\right)}_{g_{n}}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{{\rm{\|.\|_{\infty}}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}F_{g}
.

Step 1. Reformulation of the goal.
Let
\nu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
. From (
C.1
), it follows that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\widetilde{\omega}_{0}=\nu\right)=1
is satisfied with
\nu:=\omega_{t^{\star}}
. The rest of the proof is devoted to construct a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N_{4}
such that

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}}\left[F_{g}\left(\omega_{t}\right)-F_{g}\left(\omega_{s}\right)-\int_{s}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}F_{g}(\omega_{r})\mathrm{d}r}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-25.6073pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\right]=0,

is satisfied for all
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{4}
. This means that for all
0\leqslant s<t<\infty
,
A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
,
F,g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
,

\forall w\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{4},\qquad\int_{\Omega}{\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]{\mathds{1}}_{A}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)}=0,

(C.3)

where

M_{t}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right):=F_{g}\left(\widetilde{\omega}_{t}\right)-F_{g}\left(\widetilde{\omega}_{0}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}F_{g}\left(\widetilde{\omega}_{r}\right)\mathrm{d}r}.

Let
\omega\in\Omega
,
0\leqslant s<t<\infty
,
A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
,
F,g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
be fixed.

Step 2. Property (
C.3
) satisfied except on a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N_{1}(s,t,A,F,g)\in\penalty\ {\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
.
As
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}F_{g}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
, the random variable
M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}
is bounded. Note that,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}{\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]{\mathds{1}}_{A}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\hat{\omega}\right)}\left(\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]\circ\theta_{t^{\star}}\left(\widehat{\omega}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\theta_{t^{\star}}^{-1}A}\left(\widehat{\omega}\right)\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}}\left(\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]\circ\theta_{t^{\star}}{\mathds{1}}_{\theta_{t^{\star}}^{-1}A}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}\right)(\omega)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}}\left(\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]\circ\theta_{t^{\star}}{\mathds{1}}_{\theta_{t^{\star}}^{-1}A}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}+s}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-28.45274pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}\right](\omega)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}}\left[{\mathds{1}}_{\theta_{t^{\star}}^{-1}A}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}}\left(\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]\circ\theta_{t^{\star}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}+s}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}}}\hskip-28.45274pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}\right](\omega)

\displaystyle=0,

where the last equality follows from martingale property (
8
). This chain of equalities shows that the random variable
\omega\mapsto\int_{A}{\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)}
is null except on a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N_{1}(s,t,A,F,g)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
which depends on
s,t,A,F
and
g
.

Step 3. Property (
C.3
) satisfied except on a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N_{2}(s,t,F,g)\in\penalty\ {\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
.
We consider a countable subcollection
{\mathcal{E}}
of
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
which generates
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}

[
30
,
Definition 3.17 of Section 5.3 (p 306)]
and a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}-
null event
N_{2}(s,t,F,g)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
such that for
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{2}(s,t,F,g)
,

\forall A\in{\mathcal{E}},\qquad\int_{A}{\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)}=0.

Therefore, the two measures

v_{\omega}^{+}(A):=\int_{A}{\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]^{+}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)}\quad{\rm{and}}\quad v_{\omega}^{-}(A):=\int_{A}{\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]^{-}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)},

coincide on
{\mathcal{E}}
, hence on
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
. Therefore, for
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{2}(s,t,F,g)
, we have proved that for all
A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
,
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}}\left({\mathds{1}}_{A}\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]\right)=0
.

Step 4. Property (
C.3
) satisfied except on a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event
N_{3}(F,g)\in\penalty\ {\mathcal{F}}_{t^{\star}}
.
We may set now, the
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event

N_{3}(F,g):=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\,\in\,{\mathbb{Q}}\\
0\leqslant s<t<\infty\end{subarray}}{N_{2}(s,t,F,g)}.

Due to the boundedness and continuity of
t\mapsto M_{t}^{F_{g}}
, it follows from the dominated convergence theorem that for
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{3}(F,g)

\forall s<t,\forall A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{s},\quad{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}}\left({\mathds{1}}_{A}\left[M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right]\right)=0,

in other words, for all
\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{3}(F,g)
,
\left(M_{t}^{F_{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is a
\left({\mathcal{F}}_{s},{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right)-
martingale.

Step 5. Conclusion.
Now we define the
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
null event

N_{4}:=\bigcup_{F,g\,\in\,{\mathcal{B}}}{N_{3}(F,g)}

From the Step 4, we have for all
s\leqslant t
,

\displaystyle\forall\omega\in\Omega\,\backslash\,N_{4},\quad\forall A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{s},\quad\forall F,g\in{\mathcal{B}},\qquad{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}}\left[{\mathds{1}}_{A}\left(M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right)\right]=0.

From Step 0, for all
F,g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
, there exist two sequences
\left(F_{n}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}},\left(g_{n}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{{\mathbb{N}}}
such that

F_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{{\|.\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}}}F,\quad g_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{{\|.\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}}}g,\qquad{\rm{and}}\qquad{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FV}{\left(F_{n}\right)}_{g_{n}}\xrightarrow[n\to+\infty]{{\rm{\|.\|_{\infty}}}}{\mathcal{A}}F_{g}.

By the dominated convergence theorem, we deduce that for all
\omega\in\Omega
,
s\leqslant t
, and
A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}}\left[{\mathds{1}}_{A}\left(M_{t}^{F_{g}}-M_{s}^{F_{g}}\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\omega}}\left[{\mathds{1}}_{A}\left(M_{t}^{\left(F_{n}\right)_{g_{n}}}-M_{s}^{\left(F_{n}\right)_{g_{n}}}\right)\right]}=0.

which concludes the proof.
\square

C.2
Proof of Lemma
5.2

By abuse of notation, we note
h\left(\omega_{|_{\left[0,t^{\star}\right]}}\right)=h(\omega)
. We want to prove that for all
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
, for all
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}-
measurable bounded random variable
Z
,

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}\right)}\left(\left[{\mathcal{M}}_{t}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-{\mathcal{M}}_{s}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]Z\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right)=0.

Using

[
29
,
Definition 3.2 (iii)’ of Section 1
]

we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}\right)}\left(\left[{\mathcal{M}}_{t}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-{\mathcal{M}}_{s}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]Z\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right)={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}\left(\mathrm{d}\omega\right)}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}\right)}\left(\left[{\mathcal{M}}_{t}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)-{\mathcal{M}}_{s}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right]Z\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right)\right].

Thus, it is sufficient to prove that for
{\mathbb{P}}_{\nu}^{\rm FV}-
almost every
\omega\in\Omega
,
\left({\mathcal{M}}_{t}\left(\widetilde{\omega}\right)\right)_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}
is a
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\omega}\left(\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\omega}\right)-
martingale and this is what we propose to establish in the rest of this proof.

For fixed
\omega
, the function
h(\omega)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})
can be considered as deterministic.
We deduce from Theorem
C.1
